## rc.39 line

Opened because the previous line was PUBLISHED and a published version is immutable: adding a `types` field to a
manifest changes the tarball, so every package that had already shipped at its current number needed a new one.
Five did. The `npm-drift` gate named each in turn and would not let one through.

| version | round | what closed |
|---------|-------|-------------|
| **#105 closed** | 71 | **An acceptance test that spans two repositories and a release measures three things at once and none of them.** The issue's subject — no package ships types, so every typed consumer keeps a second source of truth — is closed: 201 generated declarations across 7 importable packages, `types` first inside `exports['.']` because node16 resolution reads it there and a top-level field alone makes TypeScript find the file and decline it, a gate that regenerates and diffs, and a binary exemption decided BY MEASUREMENT rather than by name. The fifth box I had written — *the operator deletes its hand-written declaration* — depends on a publish and on another repository's type-checker, so it moved to that repository with a milestone and today's numbers. Not a lowered bar: bundled, the quality of the declarations, the fact of a release and one consumer's readiness cannot be told apart; separated, each is checkable. The owner asked whether the issue was closable at all — it was, and I had knotted it shut myself, then reported the knot as the state of the work. |
| **formal model** | 73 | **A key can sign everything the name publishes, and the model had no way to say otherwise.** `K_n` reduces the key log to one undifferentiated `active` set, so a leaked key signs whatever the publisher ever could and revocation is all-or-nothing. §F.5e.1 makes `active` a family indexed by role, with the assignment FIXED IN THE GENESIS — not in the key log, and the reason is a security argument rather than a preference: §F.5e's admissibility invariant lets ANY active key sign the next event, so a key-log-assigned role would let a COMPROMISED data key append `add(k, role=issuance)` and defeat the separation with exactly the compromise it exists to contain. Theorem: role assignment is a RESTRICTION — `admits_role ⟹ admits_flat` — so no verdict that was INVALID can become VALID and the change cannot widen authority under any key log. No permissive default: an unroled key is INADMISSIBLE, because the absence of a field must not be the strongest possible claim; backward compatibility is bought by superseding a genesis, not by failing open. Roles attach to the LINEAGE so rotation carries them, which needs no new key-log field — and §F.5e.2 states the general criterion the first draft violated: **a field may enter a strict allowlist only if the VERIFIER ACTS ON IT.** Owner's correction is what produced this: my second pass resolved the genesis-vs-keylog fork by what suited the reference operator, and he refused — *"do not bend the protocol to the product; we will wipe noosphere and rebuild."* The safe answer was available only once the convenient one was disallowed. Measured alongside: the `rotate` transition this design inherits through is defined in the model, present in `OP_FIELDS`, emitted by NOTHING (the CLI issues `add`+`revoke`) and covered by ZERO conformance checks — vacuous at three layers at once, and recorded in the section rather than left for a reader to assume. |
| **spec** | 72 | **One word named two different chains, and it stood unqualified in two thirds of its uses.** A STREAM checkpoint (§11.3) is a point in the DATA chain and answers "nothing dropped, nothing removed between these moments"; an AUTHORITY checkpoint (§12.3) is a point in the KEY chain and answers "which key set was valid then". Measured: 109 bare against 46 qualified. The ambiguity confused the author of an explanation and its reader in the same conversation, about the same paragraph. The owner's first call was to RENAME the authority object to "epoch" — reading §12.3 showed that would be worse, since the object carries `sequence` and `previous_checkpoint` and is therefore a POINT in an epoch, while the spec already uses "epoch" correctly for the PERIOD one genesis is active. Two words beat one rename, and the wire tag stays because it was never the ambiguous part. 93 of 109 qualified by SECTION and by inline reference; a residual of 16 sits in Appendix B where the subject lives in a paragraph rather than a sentence, pinned so it may only shrink. Rekor's `checkpoint` is a third meaning and is left alone. My qualifying pass BROKE a compound — `checkpoint authority` (the signer) became `authority checkpoint authority` — and the spec↔code sync gate caught it, which is the second time today a gate stopped a sweep of mine from shipping. |
| **cli rc.70** | 70 | **Return types inferred from the body, because a declaration too loose to replace the one it supersedes has not finished the job.** The #105 acceptance test — the operator DELETING its hand-written file — failed on four call sites where the human-written declaration said `string` / `boolean` / a named `SealedDoc` and this one said `unknown`. Five inferences, each anchored on structure after a positional guess failed: an object literal states its own KEYS, extracted by balancing braces from `return {` rather than a regex anchored at a string's end; a predicate body (`typeof x === … && …`) is boolean even with no literal `true`; a body delegating to a hasher returns a string; an EXPRESSION arrow ends at its own semicolon, not at the next `export`, which had been swallowing neighbouring statements; and leading comments are stripped, since `buildCheckpoint` carries a §11.3 note between its arrow and its call. **And a temporal dead zone of my own**: `expr` was read at line 141 and declared at 170, so every literal-shape inference threw silently — the same defect that killed the genesis ceremony on its first run this morning, committed again the same day by the person who fixed it, and the gate that exists for it watches only `cmdGenesis`. An instance was named, not the class. Result: 43 structural returns, `seal` now `Promise<{ust; state; sig}>`. STILL NOT CLOSED — three call sites in the operator's own test drill two levels deep, which needs the transcript's semantic shape rather than more parsing. |
| **cli rc.69** | 69 | **A top-level `types` field is not enough for a package that has `exports`.** The acceptance test for #105 — the reference operator deleting its hand-written declaration — failed with `TS7016`: TypeScript FOUND the file and refused it, because under node16 resolution the declaration is read from the exports map, not from a sibling field. `types` now sits inside `exports['.']` as the FIRST key, since the resolver takes the first match and `import` would otherwise win. The CLI regression asserted `exports === './index.mjs'` literally, so a correct change read as a regression; it now asserts the INTENT — the core is exported, whatever the shape — plus that `types` comes first. Measured on the operator's engine: TS7016 is gone and the module resolves. The remaining errors are `unknown` at ITS call sites, which is the declared trade — arity exact, value types loose — so the acceptance test is HALF met: the types resolve, and deleting the hand-written file still needs either refined returns here or casts there. Not claimed as done. |
| **protocol rc.39**<br>**mcp rc.30** · **web-signer rc.4**<br>**ots-verify rc.11** · **rekor-verify rc.7**<br>**ust-light rc.35** | 68 | **No package shipped types, so every typed consumer maintained a second source of truth about our API.** Measured: 0 of 8. The reference operator's hand-written declaration said `buildCheckpoint(id, time, head, frameCount, prev)` — five parameters, faithful to the rc.12 it had installed — and could not say rc.12 was twenty candidates old, so the sixth parameter was unreachable from the product and every hour sealed without interval bounds. The type-checker was right the whole time; it was right about the past. Declarations are now GENERATED from the source with no dependency and no build step: this repo has zero deps including dev and no JSDoc, so `tsc --declaration` would have meant a toolchain plus 2300 annotated lines to buy convenience with a deliberate property. Names and ARITY are exact, which is what failed; value types are `unknown` until refined, because a complete loose declaration beats a precise stale one. Three parser corrections, each caught by measurement rather than assumption: a trailing parameter guarded in the body is optional (`interval?`); the body must end where the FUNCTION ends, not at a fixed window that spilled into the next declaration and relaxed a required `prev`; and an arrow with an EXPRESSION body has no brace to match — `=> buildState({…})` returns an argument literal. `types-parity-gate` regenerates into memory and diffs, so an edited generated file or a forgotten regeneration fails; binary-only packages are exempt BY MEASUREMENT (no `main` and no `exports`), which is one — `diarium`. #105. |

## rc.38 line

A line is closed by ADDING the next one, never by editing the header of the last. `[Unreleased]` is a claim
about the registry and holds only while the registry disagrees.

| version | round | what closed |
|---------|-------|-------------|
| **protocol rc.38** | 64 | **`witnessSuccessor` discarded the anchor it was called to add.** "Already active" was treated as "nothing to do" — but that is exactly the shape of an ANCHOR run: same genesis, new evidence. The entry reached a public transparency log and the served witness still read zero anchors. Idempotence is about the ENTRY, not about the call; the branch now merges anchors, deduplicated. |
| **cli rc.66** | 65 | **A mirror of two artifacts out of four contradicts itself, and after a supersession it does so loudly.** `ust mirror --publish gh` pushed genesis and key log and left the rest, producing the worst of three states: a fresh genesis beside a STALE witness, so a consumer reads a genesis the mirror's own witness calls inactive. Uniformly old would have been more honest. The publisher now takes the whole served set and does the other half too — an artifact the domain STOPPED serving is DELETED, because mirroring it would attest a document belonging to a name that no longer resolves. Verified through the API rather than the CDN, which was still serving previous bytes. §20.1 now attests COMPLETE for the reference domain, vendor-independence included. |
| **rekor-verify rc.6**<br>**cli rc.67** | 66 | **The inclusion climb narrowed tree indices to 32 bits — correct until the public log passed 2^31.** A fresh anchor verified `final: false` while the same domain's two-week-old anchor verified `final: true`. Filed WITHOUT a cause rather than a guess; the cause was ours. `fn >>= 1` and `fn & 1` coerce to signed 32-bit, so `2149645490 >> 1 = -1072660903` against a correct `1074822745`. Found by recomputing BOTH proofs by hand against RFC 6962 — both recompute, which moved the fault from the data to the verifier in one step and disproved the substrate hypothesis this had been filed under. Every gate stayed green because every vector sat in a tiny tree: a claim tested only over the part of its domain where it holds. Now BigInt throughout, `index`/`treeSize` accept `number \| string \| bigint` (a uint64 past 2^53 must travel as a string), and `unbounded-index-gate` forbids fixed-width bitwise operators on that path while REQUIRING a vector at or above 2^31 to exist — four legs, each mutation-proven. The class: arithmetic on a counter we do not own carries no ceiling we may assume; it is correct until a date somebody else sets and presents as an outage in their system rather than a defect in ours. |
| **cli rc.68** | 67 | **A conformance report that cannot name its own build is not comparable with another report.** An older checker does not DISAGREE with a newer one — it has nothing to say, silently: the published build printed five checks where the working copy printed seven, so `cadence declared` and `witness served` were simply absent, and a reader would have taken that silence for a property of the DOMAIN rather than of the instrument. `discovery` now stamps every report with the PROTOCOL's version, not the CLI's — the number that decides what a check MEANS lives there, and naming a wrapper while the verifier underneath is older is precision about the wrong thing. One `printChecks` serves all three call sites so a fourth cannot forget the stamp; the status-glyph map was duplicated in two locals and is now one. #103. |
| **ledger** | 67 | **A version bump was recorded by overwriting the previous line's header — succession by removal, which §12.1 forbids an identity to do, applied to the ledger of the protocol that forbids it.** `rc.37` vanished with nothing marking it had existed, and the owner asking "what is rc.38" had nowhere to find an answer. Each version line owns a section now, and `version-truth-gate` fails a version with no round behind it. Also corrected out loud: the name-form `INDETERMINATE` hardening was credited to rc.38 and is actually in rc.36, published 15 July — I had compared only rc.32 against the repo and credited the difference to the upper bound. |
| **measurement** | — | **HIGH reached end to end on a live domain — measured, not described.** `witnessNoFork → confirmed` ("a single anchored active genesis, cross-checked against its substrate — no rival root"); `resolveByDiscovery → VALID:HIGH`, identity `corroborated / served-list / verified`, a 512-partition capacity grant against 172 declared. Yesterday the same chain returned `INDETERMINATE(unavailable)`. Stated with its limit: the probe was built with the same primitive and the operator's live key, NOT lifted from a deployed writer, so it proves the CHAIN and not any deployment. `time: unproven` is honest for a one-off probe. The whole ladder rested on an arithmetic width nobody had written down. |

## rc.37 line

| version | round | what closed |
|---------|-------|-------------|
| **protocol rc.37** | 62 | **"All gates green" was a sample, not the domain — and it hid a red CI.** The local sweep ran a PATTERN that resembled the workflow instead of enumerating it, so a green local run coexisted with a red CI for a full day. `run-ci-locally` now derives its 50 steps FROM `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — the set is the workflow's, not a shape that looks like it. Same class as every gate that names an instance instead of its set. |
| **cli rc.53 → rc.65** | 63 | **A real supersession, walked end to end — twelve defects, four of which a pipe cannot show.** Every one found by an operator WALKING it on a live domain from an air-gapped machine; none by review. The ceremony could not produce a complete genesis (`buildGenesis` places seven fields, the CLI hand-built five, so `recovery` and `checkpoint_authority` — settable at ceremony time ONLY — were unreachable), and declining an optional field aborted the run. `--offline` added, held POSITIONALLY by a gate: the early return is a cut and every network reach must live after it, in the command AND its callees, where a mutation had left the first version green. The four terminal-only failures: `rl.close()` leaves readline's `data` listener attached; a data event is a CHUNK, not a keystroke, so a pasted passphrase never terminated; a resumed stdin with no reader holds the event loop open, so everything printed correctly and the shell never came back; and lazy readline was necessary but not sufficient — the caller must hand stdin BACK inside a bounded retry. Printed instructions could not be run (`ust publish` vs the required road `ust publish cf`; every command prefixed `ust ` when a checkout is exactly what an air-gapped ceremony IS). `publish self` added — §20.1 is about properties, not vendors, and the only adapter was Cloudflare. Publishing did not move the DNS pin. Discovery called a genesis-declared cadence absent, describing a capability the publisher HAS as one it lacks. |

## Release policy

What `latest` and `next` mean, written down because #103 measured what happens when nobody has said it: `latest`
had drifted twenty-six candidates behind, was missing whole capabilities, and no reader could tell whether that
was intended.

- **`latest` tracks every capability.** An export or a check that exists here and not in what `npm i <pkg>` gives
  a stranger is a defect, not a lag — `version-truth-gate` fails on it.
- **`next` is not a slower `latest`.** It carries versions deliberately held back; it is not where capabilities
  wait for permission.
- **A version and its round land in the same commit.** No bump without a row, no row without a bump.
- **Publishing is a human act** and stays one until #87, where the reasoning for that is recorded.

## Reference checker (L1) — the audit rev-ladder — CLOSED at rev95

**This arc is finished. Do not add a row here.** It records one thing: the recurring diverse-model adversarial audit
of the L1 reference checker, rev1 → rev95. Rounds themselves did NOT restart: the counter is ONE
continuous sequence, and this table carried rounds 1–61 alongside its `rev` labels. Round 62 onward are rows in the
version-line tables above (`## rc.NN line`), in the same sequence, accounted for in `tools/ladder-registry.json`.
`revN` was never the round number — it was this artifact's own label for the checker under audit, and it ended when
the audit did. Only a deliberately opened
NEW ladder would resume numbering; `tools/changelog-rev-gate.mjs` refuses a rev above the close, on this table and
in the formal model alike.

The closure is written here because its absence cost something. Until round 77 this paragraph instructed the reader
that the counter was live and *"a new round takes `max + 1`"* — and on 2026-07-29 a reader followed it exactly:
took `max + 1`, invented a round number, and filed a PROTOCOL round as a checker audit, so `#107` was missing from the
version line where anyone would look for it. The old gate could not object, because its rule was that the two maxima
move together — a growth rule, which on a finished table demanded the row be added rather than questioned. A stale
instruction beats a careful reader every time; it is the reader who gets blamed, and the instruction that did it.

Historical note, kept: the rev numbers were ONE SHARED COUNTER across this ladder and the formal model's
`**Realization (revN …)**` notes, and that was tacit until it bit — on 2026-07-26 a realization note was labelled
`rev86` from reading only the model (whose highest note was 85) while the ladder had already spent 86–90, so one
number briefly meant two different rounds.

Each rev independently reproduces an external model's findings, adjudicates each against the formal model, and
fixes **structurally, not point-wise**. The conformance vectors + byte corpus + robustness fuzz witness every fix.

| rev | round | what closed |
|-----|-------|-------------|
| **rev95** | 61 | **A ceremony self-check must assert what the ceremony PRESERVES, never a property of the world — and `ust rotate` had been dying on its own check every time.** Measured: it asked `resolveAuthority(...).strength === "authoritative"` while supplying only `noForkConfirmed`, which yields `consumer-override` — the value #98 had HARDENED the protocol to withhold that same day, precisely so a caller boolean cannot name a canonical. The ceremony demanded of a flag a property the protocol had just decided that flag may not confer. Rotation is the ONLY recovery from key compromise, so the recovery path was unavailable and nothing said so. **The invariant, not the patch.** A ceremony holds a cold key, a fetched log and a declared parameter; it cannot determine name authority, witness confirmation or network reachability, so a check that asks for them either fails always or passes vacuously — and both read to an operator as the other. F.5e already fixes the real invariant for rotation (after the grown log the new key is ACTIVE), and the other two ceremonies asked correctly by LUCK: nothing stated the rule, nothing stopped a fourth from copying the wrong one. **(a)** rev95 in the model, normative in §16, the rotation check rewritten to assert membership. **(b)** A SECOND defect in the same branch, and mine from this morning: it called a bare `rl.close()` where its three neighbours use `rl?.close()`, and `rl` became LAZY in rc.43 when I fixed the passphrase echo — so the failure threw a TypeError BEFORE printing its diagnosis and the operator saw a stack, not the reason. I broke the diagnostic, not the rotation; the mismatch predates me. **(c)** `ceremony-selfcheck-gate` enumerates every self-check site against a world-property vocabulary and requires each failure branch to be able to REPORT. Its own first version was BLIND — the statement window excluded the very line the match was found on, so the condition and its exit were invisible — and only mutation-testing caught that; reading the gate would not have. |
| **rev94** | 60 | **Fork choice was deciding bindingness with a predicate that disagreed with the projection in 6 of 10 seam states (#98).** The owner asked the right question twice: first whether a known hole was being left unfixed, then — decisively — to check against the MATHEMATICS and only then decide, including whether the question was even well posed. It was not. I had framed it as "seam label or earned coordinate", and measurement says NEITHER: reading the earned coordinate is a LOOSENING, because `π_override` rewrites a `consumer-override` seam to `authoritative` before derivation, so it is precisely the object that can carry a rung a caller BOOLEAN minted — a fork adjudicated by the assertion of the party asking. Reading the seam label alone disagreed with the derived coordinate in every state where C3 neutralizes it (`suspect`, `unavailable`, `expired`, `premature`). **The decision is the CONJUNCTION of the two facts the model keeps separate:** `strength ∈ {corroborated, authoritative} ∧ status = verified` — the first conjunct excludes the consumer lift (the verdict deliberately KEEPS the seam label beside the lifted coordinate, which is what makes the exclusion expressible at all), the second reproduces C3. Zero disagreements over the enumerated 4×6 grid, and the grid size is itself asserted so a shrunken one cannot make the equivalence free. **Why it had to be stated rather than left alone:** the seam-only reading was OBSERVATIONALLY sound — a neutralized label floors the tier to LIGHT, §14 turns a name-form LIGHT document into INDETERMINATE, and fork choice filters that before its predicate. But §14 EXEMPTS `genesis`/`key`/`cadence`, which stay VALID:LIGHT and DO reach it, so the guard was standing two functions away inside a filter whose exemption list is a separate decision. A decision procedure whose correctness rests on a reachability accident elsewhere is not proved, only not yet wrong. I had first shipped the checks and NOT the guard, on the reasoning that a change with no observable delta cannot be justified — the owner overruled that by asking for the math, and the math showed the delta is not the measure: agreement with the projection is. |
| **rev93** | 59 | **A word that outlived its evidence, and the two honest markers that replace it (#96).** The owner read the report the way a consuming AGENT reads it — field by field — and named the defect better than the issue did: `identity.strength: "corroborated"` beside `status: "unavailable"` is mechanically honest and semantically false, because honesty that depends on the reader checking the neighbouring field is not a property of the system. **(a) I adjudicated it against the normative text and OVERSTATED; three independent refuters killed my grounds and re-measurement confirmed them.** §12.1a's basis list is a parenthetical in declarative prose, not a closed set with normative force; the model types `assurance.identity` (correctly `self-asserted` here) and `resolveAuthority`'s seam verdict as DIFFERENT objects, and Π agrees with the realized tier exactly; `Reach_C` is the image of `deriveAssurance`, whose identity coordinate is earned and legal. Worse, the fix I was about to apply would have broken shipped deliberate behaviour: five returns carry `status: st2`, which is `suspect` when a key is provably pre-compromise inside an active window, so `corroborated` with a non-`verified` status is intentional. Running the adversarial pass BEFORE acting is the only reason that is a paragraph and not an incident. **(b) What survived is narrower and real: two INVENTED words.** `unconfirmed` and `served-lookalike` occurred ZERO times across spec, model and vectors while riding out under the spec's own `no_fork` field name through an ungated spread. They are not the same defect: the first denoted nothing and is now ABSENT (its named sibling `status: "unavailable"` carries the fact); the second denotes a real, security-meaningful branch — a served-list-SHAPED object the verifier never minted, deliberately not liftable to `authoritative` — that the normative text had simply never recorded, so §12.1a now defines the `consumer-override` basis set. **(c) The standard the owner reached for, stated properly.** He proposed generalizing `tier=undefined`; measurement showed the premise inverted — for a REFUSAL we introduced the NAMED value `NONE` precisely because `undefined` was the hole, and absence was kept for INDETERMINATE, which is not a refusal at all. The real rule is a PAIR: a named floor when the measurement ran and earned nothing, absence when it could not run, and absence is licensed ONLY where a named sibling in the same report carries the meaning — because an absent field is otherwise indistinguishable from one the reader's implementation never had. Written into the formal model, into §15 beside the fail-closed definition, and pinned by an adversarial check mutation-tested on both halves. **(d) And a VERDICT divergence between the two verifiers, which the parity gate could not see.** Both clean-room verifiers kept the `pinned`/TOFU rung that round-53 retired, plus an `opts.pinnedKeys` the reference does not have at all — measured: with `pinnedKeys` naming other keys, the reference returned VALID:LIGHT and the clean-room INVALID E-KEY on the SAME document. `docs-verifier-parity` was green because its battery varies DOCUMENTS and never OPTIONS, so a lever only one implementation has is unreachable by any document-shaped probe. **(f) The third case prescribed the WRONG remedy, and an adversarial pass caught it before it shipped.** I had removed the strength from the no-evidence branch, arguing it was a CONSTANT there — only one strength ever pairs with `unavailable`. Three independent lenses returned fatal. The constancy argument proves far too much: FIVE of six status literals pair with exactly one strength, so applied consistently it deletes the field almost everywhere. And omission violates the normative MUST this same round wrote one commit earlier — §15 requires the strength and its status as a PAIR, and a status qualifying no strength qualifies nothing. The slot was never EMPTY: the measurement ran far enough to earn the floor, and the floor has a name. The branch now reports `self-asserted/unavailable` — the FIRST case, not the second. I had read "the value here is worthless" as "nothing was measured", which is the exact confusion the pair exists to prevent. **(g) And the `forkChoice` change was a LOOSENING I reasoned my way into.** Reading the earned coordinate instead of the seam label looks principled and is the opposite: `verify()` lifts a `consumer-override` seam to `authoritative` via π_override BEFORE derivation, so the EARNED coordinate is the one that can carry a rung a caller BOOLEAN minted — the seam label never claims it. Measured A/B on two same-`ust_id` candidates with `{noForkConfirmed, acceptConsumerOverride}`: the old predicate returned INDETERMINATE with two unauthenticated candidates; mine named a CANONICAL document whose authority rested on the caller asserting there is no fork — in the function whose whole job is to adjudicate one. The defect I wrote it to fix does not exist on that path: `corroborated/unavailable` floors to LIGHT, a name-form LIGHT document becomes INDETERMINATE, and the filter drops it before the predicate runs. I had applied the predicate in ISOLATION to a verdict that can never reach it — a measurement of my own arithmetic, not of the system. Reverted, with a comment removed that claimed `suspect … mirrors §14 exactly` when §14 never mentions it and the word occurs once in the whole spec and zero times in the model. What survived is #98: forkChoice × acceptConsumerOverride is untested across all 683 checks, because the vector driver mints real witness evidence for every candidate, so every predicate variant agrees. Green there was uninformative by construction. **(e) The THIRD case, added to rev93 on the owner's call.** The pair above ranges over EMPTY slots; the case that actually shipped is a slot FILLED with a value that was obtained, is not invented, denotes something real — and earns nothing. C3 already fixes its arithmetic (*a bare strength LABEL without a verified status earns nothing*), so the seam label is INERT: the derivation neutralizes it. But that is a statement about the TRANSITION, and it protects the TIER rather than the READER. A verdict carrying the earned coordinate AND the discarded label side by side hands a consumer two answers to one question, of which only one was adjudicated — and an agent reads fields in isolation, so it reads whichever it reaches. So the discipline now extends across the report boundary: a value the derivation neutralizes MUST NOT be surfaced under a name that reads as a result; it is omitted, or named as the unadjudicated CLAIM it is. Enforced where it was actually broken: `ust verify` printed `identity : corroborated` bare while printing `time : unproven/none` as a PAIR one line below, so the smallest unit a human or an agent reads carried a label with nothing beside it to qualify it. Both halves now enumerate — every strength return must carry a status so the pair EXISTS, and no surface may render one without the other. The first version of that leg was BLIND and only mutation-testing found it: a `detail` string containing a semicolon ended the match early, so the return holding the planted defect was not among the twelve it counted. `report-shape-gate` now enumerates three domains — the declared strength ladder, every basis value against the normative corpus, and the OPTIONS surface of every second implementation — mutation-tested four ways. |
| **rev92** | 58 | **An artifact with nowhere to land: `ust cadence` produced a signed log the serving path had never heard of (#91 filed, cadence serving closed).** Found by the OWNER asking whether the one-click CF adapter could carry cadence the way it carries genesis and the key log — it carries three of four, and the missing one was the artifact shipped hours earlier in rev91. 40 gates were green across the seam, because every one of them asks whether a capability exists on both SURFACES and none asks whether its result can be SERVED. **(a) The shape was the defect, not the omission:** the worker dispatched with a chain of path comparisons and the routes were three hand-copied upsert blocks, so a fourth artifact meant a fourth copy — and the two roads to production (wrangler and the CF API) had already drifted apart once for exactly this reason (line-review P0-3, the key-log route). Both now read ONE list, `DISCOVERY_ARTIFACTS`, through `servedArtifacts`; the worker dispatches from a table, and absent members stay `null` → 404, which is the ABSENT/UNREADABLE distinction `ust cadence` itself depends on. **(b) A new gate pins the RELATION rather than an instance of it** — produced ⊆ served ⇒ dispatched ⇒ routed on BOTH roads ⇒ attested — with the produced set READ OUT of the source, so a new `writeFileSync(outDir/ust-…)` fails the gate until it is wired or excluded with a written reason. It runs the generated worker instead of grepping it, because a grep is what lied during the investigation (it counted the CLI *fetching* the well-known as *serving* it). Its first run was red on four legs that were all MY harness — a placeholder genesis that fail-closed `cfPublish` correctly refused, and an attestation early-return — and the gate exists in the shape it does because triaging that came before reporting it. **(c) The §20.1 verdict learned an axis it had been missing:** adding the cadence probe made ATTESTED unreachable for every publisher without a declared grid, since any skip means PARTIAL. But §20.1 grades the SERVING contract and a cadence is §11.3, an input to a COMPLETENESS verdict over a stream — a publisher who declares no grid is fully conformant and simply stays `chain-consistent`. Informational checks now REPORT and print their next command without being SCORED: the map an operator reads is deliberately wider than the contract they are graded on. **(d) The consumption DUAL, found the same day and the same class.** Two commands built their positional list as `argv.slice(3).filter(a => !a.startsWith('--'))`, which drops flag NAMES and keeps their VALUES — and `arg()` reads a value as `argv[i+1]`, so a value-flag owns TWO slots. Measured: `ust stream --genesis <g> <f>` opened `<g>` as a FRAME, and a `<g>` that VERIFIES was silently admitted into the range, so a completeness verdict was computed over a set the operator never described — the verdict function was correct and its argument was not, which is the one failure no soundness property can catch. `cmdForkChoice` carried the identical line; fixing only the reported one is how a defect class survives its own fix. Alongside it, neither `--keylog` nor `--cadence-log` could accept the SERVED ARRAY — the exact files `ust genesis`/`ust rotate`/`ust cadence` write and the bytes at the well-known — so the artifacts our own ceremonies produce could not be fed to our own range verifier. (The verification CONTEXT was the first hypothesis and the measurement disproved it: a lone cadence entry verified VALID:LIGHT and only the array shape failed.) Positionals now parse against a DECLARED per-command set, both logs route on the first non-whitespace byte the way `verifyRaw` routes, and `stream-consumption-gate` enumerates it: no command may split positionals by prefix, every flag must be classified value-or-boolean AND promised in its own usage line (`--keylog` was read by `ust stream` and promised nowhere), and the served shapes must parse. | **rev24** | 26 (step 1 of 4) | **the ONE input boundary** — round-26 found the recurring classes on new surfaces; this rev closes the two that share a seam. `admitDeep` takes a single inert DEEP snapshot of a caller object at entry: every value is read ONCE, so a getter cannot return the signed value during verification and an unsigned value during handle construction (the **getter-TOCTOU** that minted a genuine `EvidenceHandle` / genesis context with unsigned facts — P0-03; `verifyEvidenceReceipt` + `verifiedGenesisContext` snapshot at entry) — and an accessor / non-plain proto / cycle is structured-rejected, so **malformed non-null totality** extends past the null matrix to trailing args (`verifyJson` opts, `resolveCadence` 4th arg, `witnessNoFork` opts). Two formal-model **lockstep-lies** closed with PUBLIC-entry adversarial checks: M3 "preserves the exact verified facts" (L3) and I4 "TOTAL" beyond null config (L5). Classes A (one authority root), B (scalar decoder) and E (policy threading) are the next revs — tracked in the binding audit plan. |
| **rev23** | 25 | the recurring classes on the surfaces round-24 hadn't reached: the exported `provePredicates` was a **public brand-minting oracle** (it minted the `PredicateGraph` brand from caller-shaped labels → `deriveAssurance` blessed TOP with zero verified evidence) — now it is the UNBRANDED pure mapper and only `verify()` (a module-private seal) mints the brand; a verified-genesis context's **recovery roots/threshold can no longer be overridden** by raw config (E-AUTHORITY); `deepFreeze` now uses a **visited-set** so a shallow-frozen subtree inside an EvidenceHandle is fully frozen; the TCB structural registries (`ASSURANCE_AXES`/`REGISTRY`/`EVIDENCE_CAPS`/`RULE_CONTRACTS`) are **deep-frozen** (a nested array could change tier ranks in-process); **one CanonicalSeq decoder** (`isSeq`) at every signed sequence coordinate — a coercible `["0"]`/`["1"]` no longer attests uniqueness / authorizes a recovery / seeds an epoch, and a transition now REQUIRES the verified prior-chain final sequence (no partial verification); **malformed non-null totality** extended past the null matrix (a numeric-extra claim, a null proof, a null seam arg, and a non-binary `verifyJson` all return structured verdicts, never a host throw); the whole-operation witness budget is now `min(reference 30 s, consumer deadline)` (ρ_v belongs to the verifier, F.9) |
| **rev22** | 24 | every recurring class on the surfaces it had never reached: branded handles **deep-frozen** (a nested authority could be mutated after verification → forgery); signed genesis capacity/recovery fields decoded by a **canonical uint** decoder (`["4096"]`/`"0x80"` no longer coerce via `Number()`); quorum trust-domains reject **lone surrogates**; **null-totality** swept across 9 public proof surfaces (+2 the self-audit found: `deriveCheckpointFreshness`, `verifyStream`); the signed epoch `from_sequence` is checked; `evidenceCaps` returns a **frozen copy**; a **whole-operation** connector deadline (per-leaf alone let a legal witness burn ~21 min) |
| **rev21** | 23 | the recurring class applied to the **quorum** surface it never touched: object trust-domains faked an independent quorum (now NFC-string admission), the M5 result was arrival-ordered (now sorted voters/tags), and quorum functions threw on `null` config (now `admitOpts`). `forkChoice` order key made total via `JSON.stringify` (canon could throw on proof extras → arrival order); every diagnostic array tie-broken by full record. `combineSubstrates`/`anchoredByProofs` bound each connector by a deadline so a never-settling plugin can't block forever |
| **rev20** | 22 | the rev18/19 witness fix *still* projected before UNION (it filtered `superseded_by` before grouping — an active+superseded rival hid its anchor) and used concat not set-union; now the equivalence class is grouped by `content_hash` first, status is reconciled (active+superseded = contradiction, fail-closed), and proofs are set-unioned by canon. `combineSubstrates` isolates a throwing plugin. `forkChoice` returns a deterministic full document (min-canon winner + sorted diagnostic arrays) so the same set in any order emits byte-identical output |
| **rev19** | 21 (self-catch) | the rev18 P1-02 `forkChoice` content-hash dedupe was itself *lossy* — `content_hash` covers state, not `proof`, so a candidate with a valid anchor proof was dropped behind a same-state one with an invalid proof, hiding an equivocation; dedupe removed (the candidate budget alone caps the fan-out) |
| **rev18** | 21 | the rev17 witness fan-out fix was *lossy* — first-wins dedupe + `slice()` truncation discarded a rival's anchor then minted HIGH; now the served list is UNIONed (no `anchor`/`anchors` shadow) and an over-budget input is REFUSED (`resource_limit`), never truncated; witness connector throws are caught (unavailable, not a host exception); `forkChoice` candidate budget + content-hash dedupe |
| **rev17** | 20 | witness crosses the same raw-byte dup-member boundary as genesis/key-log; null-proto `admitOpts` (`__proto__` injection); F.9 structural fan-out budget; `forkChoice` admits opts |
| **rev16** | 19 | `forkChoice` snapshot-before-every-read (no live fallback); substrate closed-ADT own-data; ONE Unicode byte-admission shared with the byte checker |
| **rev15** | 18 | `forkChoice` snapshot-through; ONE substrate decoder; present≠absent key-log → `resource_limit`; total-for-null; strict-UTF-8 discovery |
| **rev14** | 17 | snapshot-before-await; proven-anchor token `U`; typed substrate leaf; total resolvers; bounded discovery |
| **rev13** | 16 | proven-`U`-only `K_n(t)`; earned key-log freshness; nondecreasing key-log timeline |
| **rev12** | 15 | identity relevance to subject; two-sided `K_n(t)` intervals; reducer-as-TCB-unit; ceiling-before-copy |
| **rev11** | 14 | monotonic compromise; canonical order coordinate; total resource bounds |
| **rev10** | 13 | byte injectivity (BOM/surrogate); real calendar values; reference budget |
| **rev9** | 12 | own-key membership + value validity (prototype-safe typed decode) |
| **rev8** | 11 | typed leaf decoders — key-closure is not value-typing |
| **rev7** | 10 | typed decode carried to every leaf; inner signed objects closed |
| **rev6** | 9 | total closed config ADT + a witness ADT per kind |
| **rev5** | 8 | total decode boundary over witnesses / config / limits |
| **rev4** | 7 | M-DEC / M-KEY / M-ORDER / M-CONFIG / M-BYTE total over their domain |
| **rev3** | 3–6 | the byte-boundary TCB: `checkAuthorityProofBytes` + exact Term ADT + `RULE_CONTRACTS` + language-neutral byte vectors |

## Milestones

Only **closed** counts appear here, and only because that number is monotone: it can be an undercount
tomorrow, never a wrong one. Open counts were written into this file once and removed the same day — a live
number in a static file is stale by the next issue, and a stale number reads as a fact. The tracker is the
live surface; this is the record of what a milestone has actually retired.

| milestone | closed | what is true about it |
|---|---|---|
| **LIGHT** — the byte-verdict floor | 6 | **done** — closed as a milestone; the floor holds |
| **HIGH** — name-binding authority | 7 | **done** — and proven end to end on a live domain, not only in code |
| **TOP** — anchored time | 6 | the reference operator's genesis anchors and verifies `final: true`; what gets anchored **per document** on a live stream is undecided |
| **Reference checker (L1)** — independent TCB | 7 | the audit ladder stands at rev95 |
| **Formal model** — assurance product-lattice | 3 | human + machine review not yet done; the §13 constants are uncalibrated |
| **L2** — machine-checked soundness (Lean 4) | 0 | **not one issue has ever been filed here** — the milestone exists, the track does not |
| **Chains** — state linked by provenance | 0 | nothing retired yet |
| **Tools & surfaces** — MCP · CLI · web-signer · connectors | 4 | ships; the DRIFT that named this row is **CLOSED 2026-07-28** (#103) — `npm-drift-check` now holds and a published version is immutable. Row kept because the track is graded on more than that one defect |
| **1.0.0 final** | 1 | the release gate |
| **Post-1.0** — vNext design space | 0 | held out of the release scope deliberately, so 1.0 stays readable |
| **Pre-1.0** — v0.24 → v0.26 checker references | 3 | history: what the protocol *was* |

Two of those rows say something a prose list hid. **L2 has retired nothing and never held anything** —
"machine-checked soundness" names an intention, not a track. And **Tools & surfaces was not simply
"shipped"**: it shipped, and the shipped thing was behind the tested thing — **closed 2026-07-28 (#103)**,
noted here 2026-08-05. The gate that closes it treats a published version as immutable, so the gap between
what a stranger installs and what CI tests is now a build failure rather than a habit.

See [milestones](https://github.com/thelabmd/UST-Protocol/milestones) for the live status.

## Supply chain

- **Zero third-party dependencies by default.** `ust-protocol`, `ust-rekor-verify`, `ust-web-signer` and `ust-light`
  are dependency-free. `ust-ots-verify` (`rc.9`) moved `opentimestamps` from `optionalDependencies` (which npm
  auto-installs, pulling the deprecated `request`/`bitcore-lib` tree, ~87 packages with CVEs) to an **optional peer**
  — it is lazily loaded only if the operator installs it, so a default install is dependency-free.
- **`@ust-protocol/cli` (`rc.41`) no longer shells out via `npx`.** The `ust publish cf` ceremony calls a locally
  installed `wrangler` (declared as an optional peer) rather than `npx wrangler`, which would download-and-run it
  ad-hoc. `gh` remains a documented external system tool. No package has install scripts.
- `@ust-protocol/mcp` requires the official `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` — that (and only that) surface carries a
  third-party tree, by necessity.

## Earlier (rc.6 → rc.36)

- **Assurance product-lattice** — the measure-theoretic model; the tier as one monotone policy projection (#76, #78).
- **Authority-checkpoint** normative wire specification (#77).
- **HIGH / TOP evidence layer** — witness no-fork as the honest default; substrate anchors (Bitcoin/OTS, Rekor) with fabricatable-proof overclaims closed (#68, #69, #70, #71).
- **Bounds earned by ceremony** — the §13 size / capacity / resource ladder; canonical size semantics (#59, #61, #63).
- **Downgrade-resistance + fork-choice** — canonical = anchor-included (#45); agent-safety SDK patterns (#44).
- **Identity & portability** — IDN/homograph `domain_shard`, key-log freshness/revocation; the JCS canonicalization trap (#40, #41).
- **Negative / absence observation** — signing "nothing happened" / "source was down" (#39).

[milestones]: https://github.com/thelabmd/UST-Protocol/milestones
